# Target blueprint 28: Traceability And Coverage (part 4 of 4)

> **Status.** Archive: proposal, not ratified. A greenfield redesign blueprint written in October 2026. Every area was Draft, Under Review or Revision Required (its own document status) and no test was run against it. Its content was mined into `docs/blueprint/`; `SSOT.md` remains the source of intent. Module federation described here was not adopted: the built system is a modular monolith (`ARCHITECTURE.md`).
>
> **What this file holds.** All files of the former `Yash_Final_Project_Document/28_TRACEABILITY_AND_COVERAGE/` folder, in order.
>
> **Merged on 2026-11-25** from the files listed below; each one is a section under its original title. Nothing was removed; the originals are in git history at commit `08387ec`.

## Legacy Reports: Evidence Index and Verification Methodology (reports group)

_Former file: `Yash_Final_Project_Document/28_TRACEABILITY_AND_COVERAGE/LEGACY_REPORTS_EVIDENCE_INDEX.md`._

Doc ID: TRC-LEGACY-C · Owner: QA and Traceability Lead (proposed) · Version 1.0 · Status: Proposed (Under Review) · Date: 2026-10-26 · Merge track: C (source group `31_LEGACY_ARCHIVE/docs/reports/**`)

**Relationship (no original file was edited).** This file **supersedes nothing**. It complements, and does not replace, the earlier crosswalks in this folder that index the same audit evidence from other angles: `AUDIT_EVIDENCE_2026-10-21_INDEX.md` (TRC-05), `AUDIT_FEATURE_EVIDENCE_CROSSWALK.csv`, `AUDIT_ROUTE_EVIDENCE_CROSSWALK.md`, `AUDIT_MIGRATION_EVIDENCE_CROSSWALK.md`, `AUDIT_TEST_EVIDENCE_CROSSWALK.md`, `AUDIT_PROBE_EVIDENCE_CROSSWALK.md`, `AUDIT_INVENTORY_EVIDENCE_CROSSWALK.md`, `AUDIT_JOURNEY_EVIDENCE_CROSSWALK.csv`, `AUDIT_EXTERNAL_ISSUE_EVIDENCE_CROSSWALK.md` (exact issue-comment bodies), `AUDIT_DOCUMENTATION_CROSSCHECK.md`, `ARCHITECTURE_DURABILITY_REPORT_CROSSWALK.md`, `PHASE_GATE_GOVERNANCE_CROSSWALK.md`. This file adds: a catalogue of all 16 files in the reports folder, the **verification methodology** the reports used, a metric timeline, the test inventory, and a structural index of the 17 top-level members of the evidence JSON with record counts. It is linked from `00_CONTROL_CENTRE/LEGACY_MERGE_LEDGER_C.md`; the documentation owner must also link it from `00_CONTROL_CENTRE/MASTER_INDEX.md`. Owner instruction behind this merge: _"i dont want to loos information keep everything in use untile it get improved"_.

**Greenfield frame.** No source code exists in this repository and nothing is implemented. The reports are dated evidence about an **earlier attempt**; their pass counts, commit ids and statuses describe that attempt's code and are not evidence about the target. The methodology (how to prove a claim) is reusable; the results are not.

Companion files: `24_RISKS_AND_DECISIONS/RISK_AND_LESSONS_FROM_PRIOR_ATTEMPT.md` (`LES-nn`), `05_FEATURE_SPECIFICATIONS/LEGACY_REQUIREMENTS_AND_ACCEPTANCE_FROM_REPORTS.md` (`LGR-nn`), `22_IMPLEMENTATION_ROADMAP/LEGACY_RELEASE_GATES.md` (`LRG-nn`).

### 2. Verification methodology used by the reports

#### 2.1 Vocabulary and rubric

- **Reality separation (SSOT rubric quoted in LEI-12 and LEI-14):** documented is not implemented; implemented is not verified; verified is not production-ready. A statement is classified by the weakest layer it has evidence for.
- **Status vocabularies.** Strict (LEI-05): Implemented and verified (zero awarded), Implemented but unverified, Partially implemented, Broken or regressed, Not implemented (search-limited), Deprecated or rejected. Earlier static (LEI-05 history): Implemented (reachable in UI, backed by API, covered by a test), Partial (some layer exists but no UI path or a subset of scope), Not started (no code found), Deprecated, Not recommended. Matrix (LEI-14): Full, Partial, Stub or mock, Missing. Plan abbreviations: IMPL, PART, NONE, DEP, NR.
- **Ten maturity dimensions per feature (LEI-07 `features[].maturity`):** UI, backend, integration, persistence, testing, security, resilience, operations, product acceptance, release readiness; each recorded with its own limitation text. No whole feature was promoted to implemented-and-verified from associations alone.
- **Three defect-evidence grades (LEI-05 F):** reproduced defect (executed probe), static failure path, evidence gap or owner decision; kept separate.
- **Honest test verdicts (from Reticle practice, LEI-02 G):** only `verified: "yes"` is a pass; `unknown` and `no-fault` are not passes; a verdict over an unrelated flow proves nothing; do not weaken a check to make it green.
- **Severity scales:** P0 to P3 (LEI-02, LEI-05); Critical, High, Medium, Low, Info (LEI-04); registry value score 0 to 10 is not urgency.

#### 2.2 Command ledger discipline (LEI-05 B)

Each check is recorded with command, current result and limitation. Examples of the pattern (results are historical): migration-history helper 5 passed (does not apply SQL); lint failed on seven files; full Vitest 1,172 passed in 115 files via a native config loader with no cache and a JSON reporter (limits: memory mode and prebuilt dependency output); non-emitting `tsc --noEmit --incremental false --composite false --declaration false --declarationMap false` per project for ten projects; install, typecheck, build, PG tests, browser tests, deployment and live email recorded as NOT RUN with the reason (protected-file rule, missing `TEST_DATABASE_URL`, no authorized target). The Vitest reporter counted 458 suites including nested `describe` blocks; 115 is the file count; do not confuse them.

#### 2.3 Layered test pyramid in the earlier attempt

| Layer               | Command (earlier attempt)                                                                                                                                           | What it proved                                                                                                    | What it did not prove                              |
| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- |
| Static              | Prettier check; `tsc -b` then `tsc -p tests`                                                                                                                        | Format; types of code and, from 2026-10-14, of the tests themselves                                               | Behavior                                           |
| Unit                | Vitest `tests/unit` (60 files, 669 tests at 2026-10-21)                                                                                                             | Domain rules, helpers, configuration                                                                              | Integration                                        |
| Integration         | Vitest `tests/integration` using in-memory Fastify `inject` (50 files, 402 tests)                                                                                   | API contracts and route handlers in memory mode                                                                   | Real database, browser, worker, provider           |
| Security            | Vitest `tests/security` (5 files, 101 tests)                                                                                                                        | Authorization, injection, RLS text, session sign-out                                                              | Hosted RLS                                         |
| Real database       | `pnpm test:pg` against an isolated `TEST_DATABASE_URL` (5 files and 23 tests on 2026-10-14; 8 files in the 10-22 report; 11 files and 50 tests in CI on 2026-10-23) | Restart round trip, race arbitration, erasure in the database, concurrency, durability labelling, token stripping | Production database, hosted roles                  |
| Browser             | Playwright on Chromium (183, 205, 221, 236 across dates) including accessibility, responsive and performance specs                                                  | User journeys, 23 pages at 7 viewports, layout shift                                                              | Other browsers, real devices, screen readers       |
| Migrations          | Node test of the ledger helper; CI applies all migrations to an empty database and re-runs the runner                                                               | Checksums, sequence, idempotency                                                                                  | Application against a populated or hosted database |
| Secrets             | Client-bundle sentinel build and scan                                                                                                                               | Server secrets not compiled into the web bundle                                                                   | Real secrets in other places                       |
| In-app verification | Reticle `reticle_act_and_wait` and `reticle_assert` against the real app                                                                                            | End states of driven flows; found three real defects                                                              | Anything not driven                                |

#### 2.4 Mutation checks and adversarial review (LEI-02 G)

Practice: for each important fix, remove or disable the fix and show the named test fails ("mutation-checked"). Recorded examples: disabling the core commit fails all core-persistence tests; removing the optimistic-concurrency guards yields 40 evaluated guesses (limit 5) and both outcomes of the hire and withdraw race; removing only the in-process guard still passes because the database guard held alone; letting a scorecard move any application fails "never resurrects"; removing per-account rate-limit keys or the health exemption fails matching tests; letting an administrator remove someone else's role fails the owner-only test; skipping the sign-out check in session resolution fails the sign-out tests; dropping the conversation start rule fails 6 messaging tests and dropping the pair lock finds two conversations; not remembering the 401 reason fails both two-device browser tests (a first mutant did not compile, so the tests ran against the previous build and passed; the mutant was rewritten). Not discriminated by any test: the lock against a retried send racing itself (a unique constraint stores one row regardless); a teardown race fix that could not be reproduced on demand. An independent reviewer agent wrote reproduction scripts; its run ended early without a written report, and its probes were rerun against pre-fix code to reproduce findings 8, 18, 19, 21 and the cross-tenant linking in 9; a memory-mode email race did not reproduce (requests happened to serialize), which is why that test lives in the PG suite.

#### 2.5 Probes (LEI-05, LEI-07 `probes`)

1. D-01 challenge text: source evaluator transpiled in memory; candidate UUID, allowed language `javascript`, one input/output case, code `this is not executable code`; result `passed`, score 100, `executed=false`, verification `unverified`.
2. D-02 batch lease: `runClaimCycle` twice on one in-memory job store; six jobs; lease 600 ms, handler timeout 300 ms, handler duration 190 ms, batch six; second worker starts after 650 ms; result 6 jobs, 9 executions, 3 duplicated. Defaults (batch ten, lease 60 s, timeout 30 s, mail timeout 20 s) share the structural risk; this proves duplicate handler execution under synthetic timing, not duplicate real mail (the adapter retains a provider idempotency key). Recommended next test: a PG claim-generation test before changing the store contract; measure heartbeat aging with the same drill.
3. D-03 (not run): inject a rejection of the metadata-erase step after the core erasure commit; the account is already deactivated.

#### 2.6 E2E audit design (LEI-04)

Isolated stack: API on :4100 (`STORAGE=memory`, pinned token secret, raised rate limits) and web dev server on :5199 proxying to it; a stale shared API on :4000 was deliberately not used. Accounts: two candidates, a recruiter, a platform administrator promoted with an ops token. Browser: Chromium desktop 1280x900 and mobile 390x844; the Reticle overlay produced extra dialogs and unlabeled buttons that were counted as tooling artifacts. Results by suite:

| B     | Sign-up and login validation, enumeration, XSS in name, open redirect, double submit, session, logout                         |  20 |     18 |            2 |                      0 |
| C     | API authorization: admin routes, token tampering, privilege escalation, IDOR, headers, CORS, payload limits, admin governance |  53 |     52 |            1 |                      0 |
| D     | Admin console in the browser                                                                                                  |  23 |     20 |            1 |                      2 |
| E     | Candidate flows                                                                                                               |  31 |     24 |            2 |                      5 |
| E2    | Re-probes of E failures                                                                                                       |  10 |      5 |            4 |                      1 |
| F     | Recruiter: job validation, publish, XSS in titles, two-tab race                                                               |  14 |     12 |            0 |                      2 |

First pass 227 checks, 191 passed, 36 failed; after fixes 13 of 13 regression checks and a full suite of 113 files and 1,159 tests. Scripts, raw outputs and screenshots lived in a session scratchpad and are not in the repository. Routes exercised: public (`/`, `/login`, `/signup`, `/checkout`, `/privacy`, `/terms`, `/jobs`, `/jobs/:id`, `/reference/:id`, unknown path), candidate (`/dashboard`, `/applications`, `/evidence`, `/profile`, `/settings`, `/portfolio`, `/resumes`, `/network`, `/learning`, `/notifications`, `/messages`, `/messages/:id`, `/assessments`), hiring (`/hiring`, `/hiring/jobs/:id`), admin (`/admin`); about 240 API routes existed, most without UI.

#### 2.7 Boundaries stated by the reports (read before trusting any number)

- Static route and file search, test-title feature ids and web API-call inspection were the basis of the early status; "Not started" means no matching code was found, so a feature with a different name could exist.
- Coverage by subsystem (LEI-05 B): authority and product documents (registries indexed completely; the long SSOT not read line by line); architecture (core paths traced; no review of all 13,356 server lines); web (source and helper tests; no live UI, keyboard, responsive, contrast or screen-reader validation); persistence (core transaction, guard, hydration, writer lease, stores; applied schema, hosted grants, restart and concurrency unverified); worker and integrations (claim loop, mail adapter, event registry, heartbeat; mocks only); quality and release (CI definition inspected; no current remote job logs); history and planning; remaining portfolio (source and test feature-id references and domain filename candidates, not semantic proof).
- LEI-11 inspected a 978-entry tree (859 tracked files) at inventory level only; detailed review was targeted to billing, migrations, security, E2E and high-risk modules; hosted CI results were not re-executed locally.
- Inventory coverage in the JSON: 684 tracked files, of which 358 "inventoried; not necessarily inspected" and 326 "indexed for ID references; only narrative-named paths deeply reviewed".
- Files absent from the audited checkout: `ALL_DOCUMENTS.md`, `FINAL_DOCUMENT_INDEX.md`, `docs/engineering/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/features/`; the Bash maintenance tool and its fixture tests.

### 7. Evidence rules the reports converge on (reusable by the target)

1. Record the exact commit, date, runtime versions and whether the working tree was clean; if another actor changes the tree mid-audit, say so and describe the evidence as "the working source observed during that transition".
2. Separate three kinds of finding: reproduced by an executed probe, a static failure path, and an evidence gap or owner decision.
3. A command that was not run is "NOT RUN" with its reason; never imply a pass. A test that exits 0 because a tool is missing (a swallowed `grep` failure) is not evidence.
4. Test counts name their unit: tests, files and nested suites are different numbers.
5. Memory-mode green does not certify database behavior; a database green does not certify a hosted platform; CI green does not certify a deployed environment; a mocked provider call does not certify a provider.
6. Prefer a mutation check: remove the fix, show the test fails; prove the mutant compiles.
7. Run at least one real-browser, real-database, real-limits path per release; a suite that raises the limit it should test hides defects.
8. Tests that import built output record the build provenance; a stale build can fail or pass for the wrong reason.
9. Historical results carry their banner and are never re-labelled as current; a later audit states which earlier statements it supersedes.
10. Candidate associations (a table, a route, a similar page, a comment naming a rule) are not proof of implementation or of requirement coverage.
11. For every external system (payment provider, managed database, mail provider, hosting) record read-only observations separately from mutations; the 2026-10-23 review made none.
12. A recommendation that needs a protected or owner-controlled change (dependency manifest, build configuration, pricing, legal text) is a proposal until the owner authorizes that path.

### 10. Selected end-to-end flow traces recorded on 2026-10-21 (FEATURE_STATUS D)

Each trace is UI, then API and authorization, then domain, then storage and asynchronous work, then outcome. None is a certification; J-05 to J-28 not covered were explicitly unverified.

| Journey                             | Trace                                                                                                                                                                              | Evidence and limits recorded                                                                                                                                                           |
| ----------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Identity and session (supports all) | Sign-up, login, profile pages; auth and profile routes with session extraction and resolution; auth and profile domain; users, profiles, revocations in the core store; session UI | `auth-profile`, `session-sign-out` and production-config tests passed in memory; PG account authority differs from test token shortcuts; browser reload and multi-tab not run          |
| Work-history proof (F-94, F-162)    | Evidence and reference pages; owner check, mailbox challenge, token check; work-history graph domain; core store plus queued mail; badge and graph                                 | Challenge hashed, expiring, bounded attempts; referee token mandatory and consumed; mocked mail adapter and authorization tests passed; real mailbox, PG race and restart not executed |
| J-02 apply and track                | Jobs, job detail, applications; publish, ownership and eligibility checks; jobs and applications domain; guarded core transaction plus notification; application status            | API tests passed; PG BR-15 duplicate and reapply tests exist but not run; full interview, offer, outcome terminal not proven                                                           |
| J-03 and J-04 recruiter             | Hiring pages; organization membership and role checks; job and application transitions; core plus notifications; pipeline                                                          | Subset supported; organization lifecycle, scorecards, interviews and actual hire acceptance not fully traced                                                                           |
| Communication                       | Messages, conversation, notifications; participant and recipient checks; messaging and notifications domain; core transaction; thread and badge                                    | Memory tests passed; dedicated PG tests exist; browser delivery, reload, multi-user races and external notification delivery unverified                                                |
| Account deletion                    | Profile and settings; session and erasure routes; settings anonymization; core commit, then separate queue, account, bookmark, search and request operations; deactivation         | Request, status, cancel durable; cross-store failure after deactivation can strand cleanup (D-03); no due-date executor or approved retention and backup policy                        |
| J-01 and J-19 learning and proof    | Learning and course pages; enrollment and prerequisite checks; LMS domain; process-local module, lesson, progress, certificate state; evidence in the core store                   | Helper and API tests passed; reload within one process is not restart durability; no full Learn, Prove, Showcase acceptance                                                            |
| J-26 career                         | Career, skills, career-paths components; readiness, relationship, transition APIs; career and skills rules; mixed ephemeral graph data                                             | UI source existed; model validity, cohort boundaries, persistence and full transition outcome unverified                                                                               |
| Monetization                        | Checkout shows plans; billing gate defaults disabled; billing domain; simulated process state only if permitted                                                                    | Production simulation refusal tested; no live payment flow                                                                                                                             |

Architecture assessment of the same date (condensed): React/Vite to Fastify to shared contracts and domain to PostgreSQL plus an in-process read model, plus a separate PostgreSQL job worker; core operations committed before maps are updated; hydration reads the core dataset at boot (volume limits unmeasured); static route count 274 registrations, 70 durable-registry entries, 3 stateless, 201 ephemeral-header routes, which are labels and not 70 proven durable workflows; 51 SQL files; the AI assistant was a local heuristic and two assistant routes call the assessment-AI check; browser sessions were bearer tokens in storage; no rewrite or route-module split was justified merely by server size; profile measured boot and hot paths before changing the persistence design.

Matrix recommendations of 2026-10-22: the 26 "Full" features formed the closed core loop (identity F-01, 03, 12, 15, 60, 65, 80; skills and evidence F-84, 96, 173; proof of control F-58, 94, 162; marketplace and hiring F-04, 05, 06, 25, 32, 56, 102, 117; communication F-10, 14, 29; resilience F-02, 21); durability migration order for partial features F-08, F-07, F-36, F-26, F-17. Several of those "Full" labels were later rated partial or not implemented (lessons LES-06).

````text
L1: # TalentSphere — 173 Canonical Features Master Traceability Matrix
L2:
L3: > Historical report: findings, counts and executed checks below belong to the recorded revision/session. Current source references and inspection limits are in the implementation inventory (`../system-model/01-inventory-and-coverage.md`, not present in this tree). No historical pass is a fresh release certification.
L4:
L5: **Date:** 2026-10-22
L6: **Author:** Teamwork Master Synthesis Engineering Group
L7: **Repository:** `https://github.com/yhsomani/talentsphere-complete.git`
L8: **Git HEAD:** `83dc7d247efbd3b3c3312bef74ac49978d33e924`
L9: **Active Branch:** `repository_audit_roadmap`
L10: **Authoritative Baselines:** `SSOT.md` v6.0, `FEATURE_REGISTRY.md` v6.0, `ADR-015`, `PRD.md`, `TRD.md`
L11:
L12: ---
L13:
L14: ## 1. Executive Summary & Methodological Rubric
L15:
L16: An exhaustive, code-level audit was conducted across all **173 canonical features** (F-01 through F-173) defined in the TalentSphere capability registry (`docs/registries/FEATURE_REGISTRY.md`). Every feature was cross-referenced against:
L17: 1. **Fastify API Routes** in `apps/api/src/server.ts` (274 HTTP route registrations).
L18: 2. **Domain Models & Services** in `packages/domain/src/` (48 TypeScript modules).
L19: 3. **Frontend UI Routes & Components** in `apps/web/src/` (20 pages, 16 components).
L20: 4. **Database Migrations** in `supabase/migrations/` (49 PostgreSQL migrations).
L21: 5. **Durability Classifications** pursuant to ADR-015 and runtime header enforcement (`x-talentsphere-durability`).
L22:
L23: ### Classification Rubric (Reality Separation)
L24: In accordance with SSOT §Reality Separation:
L25: ```text
L26: DOCUMENTED ≠ IMPLEMENTED
L27: IMPLEMENTED ≠ VERIFIED
L28: VERIFIED ≠ PRODUCTION-READY
L29: ```
L30:
L31: - **Full (26 features / 15.0%)**: End-to-end implementation. Relational schema in PostgreSQL migrations, pure business logic in domain packages, Fastify route in `apps/api/src/server.ts` running in durable storage mode (`DURABLE_ROUTES`), dedicated interactive React UI component/page in `apps/web/src/`, and automated test coverage (including Playwright E2E).
L32: - **Partial (91 features / 52.6%)**: Substantial code implementation. Domain models, database migrations, and Fastify endpoints exist and pass unit/integration tests, but state is currently in-memory (`ephemeral`) and/or frontend UI is missing or preview-only.
L33: - **Stub / Mock (6 features / 3.5%)**: Deprecated legacy endpoints (F-38, F-39) or explicitly vendor-gated/prohibited concepts (F-45, F-61, F-62, F-63).
L34: - **Missing (50 features / 28.9%)**: Deferred to future roadmap phases (Phases 5–10), Chrome Extension companion, or unbuilt enterprise modules.
L35:
L36: ---
L37:
L38: ## 2. Capability Portfolio Distribution
L39:
L40: ```text
L41: ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

L47: │ Partial               │ 91        │ 52.6%        │ Built in domain & API; ephemeral/UI │

L50: ├───────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤

L54:
L55: ### Durability Breakdown Across Routes
L56: - **Durable (54 registered routes, 28 features)**: Writes persist to PostgreSQL via `DURABLE_ROUTES`. Single-writer advisory lock lease (`pg_try_advisory_lock(718201, 1)`). Verified by Playwright E2E and real-DB integration tests.
L57: - **Ephemeral (217 registered routes, 87 features)**: State maintained in ~150 in-process `Map` collections. Responses return `x-talentsphere-durability: ephemeral`. Wiped on server restart.
L58: - **Stateless (3 registered routes, 3 features)**: Diagnostics and static plan catalogs (`/health`, `/api/v1/health`, `/api/v1/billing/plans`).
L59: - **Non-API / Future Scope (55 features)**: Features without direct HTTP route handlers in `server.ts`.
L60:

L243: ## 4. Architectural Analysis & Durability Recommendations
L244:
L245: ### 4.1 The Core Career Loop Foundation (26 Full Features)
L246: The 26 **Full** features form a cohesive, production-grade closed loop:
L247: 1. **Identity & Authentication**: F-01, F-03, F-12, F-15, F-60, F-65, F-80.
L248: 2. **Skills & Evidence**: F-84, F-96, F-173.
L249: 3. **Verifiable Proof of Control**: F-58, F-94, F-162.
L250: 4. **Marketplace & Hiring Pipeline**: F-04, F-05, F-06, F-25, F-32, F-56, F-102, F-117.
L251: 5. **Direct Communication**: F-10, F-14, F-29.
L252: 6. **Platform Resilience**: F-02, F-21.
L253:
L254: This core loop is backed by **PostgreSQL 16**, fully typechecked, and verified by Playwright browser tests.
L255:
L256: ### 4.2 Progressive Ephemeral Migration Priorities (Stage C)
L257: The 91 **Partial** features already possess well-defined domain logic and database migrations in `supabase/migrations/`. To eliminate ephemeral runtime data loss, they should be migrated to PostgreSQL storage in order of business value:
L258: 1. **F-08 Challenges & Assessment**: Connect `00004_challenges_assessment_schema.sql` to `storage.core.persist()` and wire `AssessmentsPage.tsx` to active endpoints.
L259: 2. **F-07 LMS & Courses**: Route enrollment, course completion, and certificate minting through PostgreSQL.
L260: 3. **F-36 Application Draft Autosave**: Persist drafts to `application_drafts` table to prevent candidate data loss.
L261: 4. **F-26 Portfolio Showcase**: Wire project records to `portfolio_projects` table.
L262: 5. **F-17 Admin Console**: Build a React admin dashboard and persist platform configuration and feature flag overrides.
L263:
````

## Observed Implementation Control Crosswalk

_Former file: `Yash_Final_Project_Document/28_TRACEABILITY_AND_COVERAGE/OBSERVED_IMPLEMENTATION_CONTROL_CROSSWALK.md`._

Doc ID: TRC-07 | Owner: Documentation Architect | Version: 1.0 | Status: Candidate preservation map; owner review required | Date: 2026-10-25

This crosswalk maps dated reference claims in four repository documents to candidate target documents. It separates implementation observations, source requirements, historical test results, stale prose, and future target design. It is not a code audit, current test report, adopted requirement, or approval. Every row remains an unapproved candidate. The companion [CSV](#observed_implementation_control_crosswalkcsv) preserves 38 row-level claims, source document/evidence IDs, revision and content hash, anchors, target candidates, comparison labels, and unresolved disposition.

### Sources and provenance

| Source                                                               | Manifest record         | Source revision                                                  | Content SHA-256                                                    | Scope note                                                                                   |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| [`docs/engineering/DATABASE.md`](../../engineering/DATABASE.md) | DOC-000137 / EVD-000137 | `431c425a`, 2026-10-23                                           | `0879ff0703827fa3253380daca3232a45f78407fe5f21e31f92e8706a4915f82` | Static storage, migration, privacy and RLS observations; no live DB proof.                   |
| [`docs/quality/OPERATIONS.md`](../../quality/OPERATIONS.md)     | DOC-000152 / EVD-000152 | `2aea1085`, 2026-10-24; current-build section checked 2026-10-23 | `f4977e0a82c853064fd27137f03b8ae7fc836cba68955f6fb6b2b01c81627c18` | Sections 1-8 are a target operating model; section 9 is reference implementation/operations. |
| [`docs/quality/SECURITY.md`](../../quality/SECURITY.md)         | DOC-000153 / EVD-000153 | `2aea1085`, 2026-10-24                                           | `dda6537adf773b945e8fbd2ffae16e050902d0ae76bc392a811df6a100bb7006` | Source limits evidence scope and distinguishes requirements from assurance.                  |
| [`docs/quality/TESTING.md`](../../quality/TESTING.md)           | DOC-000154 / EVD-000154 | `335e9827`, 2026-10-23                                           | `fbe7077f12d7ea0893fdc04a3a2d88b35ca3a93a4368e74fc1882df9252b2f11` | Historical baseline is dated 2026-10-21; coverage claims are not current test results.       |

### Interpretation

- `candidate-partial`, `candidate-conflict`, and `candidate-stale-reference` describe the comparison in named target files; they do not select future policy.
- `candidate-historical-evidence` preserves dated results. `candidate-covered` means a similar control or caveat exists, not that implementation is verified.
- `VerificationState` is conservative: current code/runtime was not revalidated for these rows in this checkpoint. The four source Markdown files were read and content hashes recorded.
- Repeated topics (erasure, RLS, delivery and export) retain distinct source evidence; they are not independent proof. Target design remains subject to owner review. Root `SSOT.md` remains unchanged.

### Principal unresolved dispositions

1. Reconcile single-writer API topology, Map-backed durability, worker duplicate execution and target outbox/fencing design.
2. Complete table/module durability and erasure/export inventories, retry behavior, legal holds, shared-record retention and deletion evidence.
3. Map source RLS/grant limits to target tenant policies and define evidence beyond `RLS enabled`.
4. Map provider acceptance versus delivery, queue retry/idempotency and integration incident handling.
5. Keep authentication, hashing, session and billing reference behavior separate from target designs; verify current claims against code when required.
6. Separate historical tests from target test specifications; close accessibility, performance and release-evidence gaps.
7. Route the `z.coerce.boolean()` discrepancy to a separately authorized code task; this initiative does not change source code.

### Validation limits

CSV structure and row IDs can be checked mechanically; source anchors can be compared to current text. No database query, migration, app test, browser, deployment, live provider, legal review or production control was exercised. Source Markdown and target design documents remain in place; no cleanup is authorized by this map.

## PHASE_GATE_GOVERNANCE_CROSSWALK.md

_Former file: `Yash_Final_Project_Document/28_TRACEABILITY_AND_COVERAGE/PHASE_GATE_GOVERNANCE_CROSSWALK.md`._

﻿# Phase, Gate and Governance Preservation Crosswalk

Status: **candidate mapping; no approval established**. This document and the [69-row CSV](#phase_gate_governance_crosswalkcsv) preserve source intent and identify target candidates. They do not change execution order, admit scope, ratify architecture, approve a pilot or GA, or establish implementation.

### Authority and phase vocabularies

The source [SSOT](../../../SSOT.md#L5-L10) defines intended behavior; executable evidence determines implementation and readiness. [Governance](../../governance/GOVERNANCE.md#L3-L22) assigns the implementation plan execution-order responsibility and keeps current architecture separate from intended architecture.

Keep these three vocabularies separate:

| Vocabulary                       | Source location                                                             |                                      Rows |
| -------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------: |
| IP founder execution phases 0–10 | [Implementation plan](../../governance/GOVERNANCE.md#L19-L52) |                                        11 |
| SSOT capability phases Ph0–Ph10  | [SSOT §23.1](../../../SSOT.md#231-phases-010)                                  |                                        11 |
| RDM delivery phases Ph0–Ph4      | [Target roadmap §4](22_IMPLEMENTATION_ROADMAP.md#L71-L79)        | 5 target phases, referenced as candidates |

Matching phase numbers do not establish equivalence. The implementation plan's [current-baseline note](../../governance/GOVERNANCE.md#L3-L17) points to the current audit and delivery roadmap and preserves the distinction between a proposed restricted beta and career-loop GA. The target [roadmap](22_IMPLEMENTATION_ROADMAP.md#implementation-roadmap) and [developer guide](29_DEVELOPER_IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDE.md#developer-implementation-guide) are draft planning artifacts.

### Preserved source rows

| CSV family              | Source rows |
| ----------------------- | ----------: |
| ip_phase                |          11 |
| ssot_phase              |          11 |
| ip_exit_gate            |           8 |
| ip_epic                 |          20 |
| approval_class          |           7 |
| ssot_mvp_exit           |           7 |
| ssot_institutional_exit |           5 |
| **Total**               |      **69** |

The eight IP gates apply to every founder phase. IP epic IDs are source ordinals, not new EPIC identifiers. MVP and institutional exits were extracted only within their own source sections. Exact source labels, scopes and exit criteria are preserved in separate CSV fields; source statements retain the inspected Markdown text.

Source anchors are line locators in the named source files at inspection time. Target anchors use L for roadmap lines, § for roadmap sections and DEV L for developer-guide lines. These locators can move when documents change. Candidate target IDs are existing roadmap work-package IDs.

### Reading the mappings

- **split**: candidate coverage spans multiple target packages or phases.
- **partial**: a candidate touches the source requirement, with the remaining gap stated explicitly.
- **unmapped**: no matching preservation rule was identified in the two inspected targets; a nearby target section may still be listed to explain the gap.

Every row remains candidate-unapproved, with approval evidence not-established. The target has 60 work packages, 15 initiatives, 15 workstreams and seven release-gate rows; those counts do not substitute for the source's phases, eight universal exit gates or 20 ordered epics.

The seven C classes preserve both the [governance classification](../../governance/GOVERNANCE.md#L28-L38) and [SSOT approval requirements](../../../SSOT.md#2482-change-classes-c1c7). ADR/ARB escalation in the target developer guide does not establish Founder approval or the source reviewer counts.

### Controls outside the 69-row inventory

Retain the [dependency rule](../../governance/GOVERNANCE.md#L89-L91): blocking dependencies must be ready or intentionally mocked with a documented exit path. Preserve [register-integrity ratification](../../../SSOT.md#2483-register-integrity-invariant), [documentation sync](../../governance/GOVERNANCE.md#L67-L69), and [historical-source restrictions](../../governance/GOVERNANCE.md#L71-L73). The source Ph10 exclusions and Founder gate remain active source requirements.

### Validation and limits

CSV parse and round-trip checks passed for 69 unique family/source identifiers and the seven category counts above. Every candidate WP ID exists as a table-row ID in the inspected target roadmap. Source hashes were compared before writing.

Only this CSV and companion Markdown were created. No tests, builds, source changes, schema migrations or runtime verification were performed. Security and commercial approval requirements are preserved as source requirements; no approval is claimed. The mapping is limited to the two named target documents and is not proof of wider project coverage or implementation readiness.

## Route Authorization Finding Crosswalk

_Former file: `Yash_Final_Project_Document/28_TRACEABILITY_AND_COVERAGE/ROUTE_AUTHORIZATION_FINDING_CROSSWALK.md`._

Doc ID: TRC-04 | Status: Candidate preservation map; owner review required | Date: 2026-10-25

The [CSV crosswalk](#route_authorization_finding_crosswalkcsv) preserves CF-31 through CF-45 from `docs/system-model/06-route-authorization.md`, including each finding, evidence anchor, source rationale, candidate target documents, and related open decisions. The source labels these findings open in its review; this map does not establish that any defect is present in current code. The review says claims are reported unless marked Fixed or Tested, and routes outside its listed groups were not read. Revalidate implementation and tests before changing security or product behavior.

The candidate target links organize follow-up only. They do not imply that target policy has been approved or that the source finding has been resolved. OPEN-44, OPEN-48 and OPEN-50 retain the related authorization and traceability decisions.

## Canonical SSOT Feature Crosswalk (Draft)

_Former file: `Yash_Final_Project_Document/28_TRACEABILITY_AND_COVERAGE/SSOT_FEATURE_CROSSWALK.md`._

**Status:** Candidate mapping for owner review; OPEN-41 remains open.  
**Sources:** root `SSOT.md` §7 (173 canonical `F-*` identities) and `04_PRODUCT_CAPABILITIES/CAPABILITY_MAP_AND_FEATURE_HIERARCHY.md` (253 local `FEAT-*` rows).  
**Authority:** This is a traceability aid, not a new source of product scope, phase assignment, or implementation status.

### Method and limits

Each canonical feature was compared with local feature titles and behavior notes. `D` means a direct name/behavior overlap; `P` means a partial overlap or one component of a broader canonical feature; `—` means no defensible local match was found. A match is not a claim of complete coverage or implementation. Several F-to-FEAT mappings are intentionally one-to-many and many canonical identities have no candidate. The entries are proposed and need product-owner review before portfolio coverage or implementation readiness can be claimed.

The phase systems are not comparable: SSOT §7 uses its own `Ph0`–`Ph10` sequencing, while the capability map's local `P0`–`P4` values are proposed product sequencing. This crosswalk does not translate or reconcile phases. Feature dispositions such as `Remove`, `Defer`, and `Introduce` remain as written in the local map and require their own owner decisions where applicable.

- Confirm every proposed link and whether the local row is an acceptable decomposition of the canonical scope; `P` entries especially need confirmation.
- Confirm whether canonical features marked deprecated or not recommended should map to local rows marked `Remove` (F-38, F-62, F-63).

The table contains one row for each canonical F-01 through F-173 identity. Candidate links are based on document names and behavior notes only. ## SSOT_RECONCILIATION_AUDIT.md

_Former file: `Yash_Final_Project_Document/28_TRACEABILITY_AND_COVERAGE/SSOT_RECONCILIATION_AUDIT.md`._

﻿# Protected Source and Target Reconciliation Audit

Doc ID: TRC-02 · Status: Draft for owner review · Date: 2026-10-25

This report preserves high-impact differences found while comparing the protected root `SSOT.md` and related legacy source evidence to the proposed blueprint. It records traceability work still required; it does not approve the target, retire a source requirement, or establish implementation. Root source line references are anchors in the audited version and can shift if the protected source changes.

### Decision and map index

| Workstream                             | Evidence and observed delta                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Required disposition                                                                                                          |
| -------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Feature identity                       | SSOT `F-01…F-173`; candidate links in [SSOT feature crosswalk](#canonical-ssot-feature-crosswalk-draft) include gaps and unrepresented identities.                                                                                                               | OPEN-41; product owner approves or revises candidate links.                                                                   |
| Rules and invariants                   | `BR-001…248` and `MIG-001…008`; 256 identities, four text conflicts, 171 no-match in the source-aware [legacy rule crosswalk](06_BUSINESS_PROCESSES.md#legacy-business-rule-crosswalk).                                                                  | OPEN-43; do not infer retirement from no-match.                                                                               |
| Journeys and workflows                 | `J-01…28`, `WF-01…66`, `WIT-001…025`; candidate journey map and 91-row WF/WIT map are partial, with duplicate WF-33/WF-53 in source.                                                                                                               | OPEN-45; state-machine mapping remains open.                                                                                  |
| API/CON/SCI                            | The 54-row [SCI crosswalk](13_API_EVENTS_AND_INTEGRATIONS_PART2.md#ssot-service-contract-crosswalk) and 16-row [CON crosswalk](13_API_EVENTS_AND_INTEGRATIONS_PART2.md#ssot-interface-contract-crosswalk) are candidate maps, not approved dispositions. | OPEN-46 and OPEN-50; owner-review both candidate maps and resolve envelope, pagination, deprecation, and interface conflicts. |
| Domain boundaries                      | Source modular-monolith domain ownership differs from target groupings; federation remains an optional target proposal.                                                                                                                            | OPEN-47; owner records boundary disposition and mode decision.                                                                |
| Security, privacy, and AI              | Material policy differences below; source values are not automatically adopted.                                                                                                                                                                    | OPEN-48; security/privacy/AI owners and counsel where applicable.                                                             |
| Reliability and operations             | Queue, realtime, performance, release, and recovery controls differ below.                                                                                                                                                                         | OPEN-49; SRE/Platform owners map source controls to target evidence and calibrate.                                            |
| Phases, governance, UX, tail registers | Stable source IDs and several contradictory inventory counts lack row-level dispositions.                                                                                                                                                          | OPEN-50; retain source IDs and contradictions pending owner resolution.                                                       |

### API, integration, queue, realtime, and media contracts

Root SSOT §13 (§13.1–§13.14 in v6.2 numbering) define a 54-entry service contract register, integration removal drill VER-021, separate transactional and heavy queues, and detailed realtime/media behavior. Current target architecture proposes materially different defaults or leaves the contract as a placeholder.

- **SCI-01…SCI-54:** a 54-row candidate crosswalk now retains each operation, actor, idempotency key, candidate API ID, and residual. It records 33 partial candidates and 21 operations with no specific target catalog match. Candidate links are not approved equivalence; source SCI-09…16 (institutional licensing, import, seat operations, media authoring and progress), SCI-19…23 (course versions, review, assignments and peer review), SCI-25 (migration abort/rollback), SCI-27 (payout), SCI-34 (newsletter), SCI-38…39 (assessment/recording), and SCI-45…54 (emerging skills, relationship validation, introductions, feedback, freshness, referrals and alumni) retain material gaps. Names or domain similarity are not coverage evidence.
- **Integration removal:** source VER-021 gates every integration-touching release on a removal drill; target proposes semiannual game days. Decide whether the per-release requirement, target cadence, or a revised risk-tiered policy governs.
- **Queues:** source separates Postgres transactional work (enqueue under 10 ms; process under 5 s; 14 listed job kinds) from heavy durable jobs (9 listed kinds). Target proposes one Postgres fenced-worker engine. Reconcile workload isolation, retry/DLQ, latency, and failure behavior before API/backend contract freeze.
- **Realtime:** source specifies authenticated/RLS-projected channels, payloads ≤4 KB, monotonic cursors, 50 events/s/channel and overflow refresh; launch capacity target is ≤2,500 concurrent connections/40 topics. Target SSE/15-second polling approach does not yet map these contracts or capacities.
- **Media:** source defines MediaSource/adapter/player, five delivery modes, 15 provider capabilities, and AV/transcode/HLS/thumbnail/caption/CDN processing and security. Target `MediaPort` is only a placeholder. Preserve the detailed source scope until an owner explicitly narrows or replaces it.
- **Integrations:** source proposes Supabase substrate, Resend/Postmark, named OAuth, heuristic-first then LLM AI, user-authorized provider delegation, observability, and later HRIS/LMS/media connections. Target proposes PostgreSQL, Brevo, generic identity provider and API-key AI. These are target choices to review, not source facts.

### Security, privacy, and AI controls

Root SSOT sections 15-17 and 31-32 (approximately lines 3591-3885) differ from target security/privacy/backend/assessment contracts in the following decision-relevant areas:

- **Remediation:** source critical <24 h with patch ≤72 h; high <72 h or a seven-day path with blocking controls; medium <14 days. Target allows high up to 14 days and medium up to 60 days. Set severity-specific owner-approved SLAs.
- **AI redaction and evidence:** source redaction covers full names and street addresses and includes company-exclusion/display-restoration behavior. Source also calls for Unicode cleanup, canary-leakage checks, capability-specific suppression/display, evidence/assumption provenance, and evaluation gates. Target coverage is narrower or partial. Do not treat generic PII redaction as equivalent.
- **Provider access:** source distinguishes connected provider/API rights and an order of local processing, free quota, explicitly authorized provider, permitted cloud, then non-AI fallback. Target does not fully carry those distinctions. Resolve under OPEN-24 and OPEN-48; no free-tier cost entitlement is implied.
- **Assessment AI:** source includes four modes, including `POST_ASSESSMENT_ONLY`; target feature spec has two modes, data model three, and backend policy restrictions. Align the lifecycle contract before implementing or claiming assessment AI.
- **Residency and erasure:** source fixes US-East and describes a Phase 3 EU partition, EU AI egress/fallback, and transfer controls; target leaves region undecided. Source FERPA graduation severance is more specific than target generic erasure. Resolve region under OPEN-08/30 and preserve a distinct graduation disposition.
- **RLS:** source says every table is RLS-protected; target physical design exempts internal tables. Reconcile service-role access, tenant isolation, migration policy, and canary coverage. Avoid the broad `RLS everywhere` claim while exemptions remain.
- **Compliance:** source includes explicit commitments; target leaves counsel confirmation open. Treat all applicability statements as provisional until legal review.

### Reliability, release, and service operations

Root SSOT sections 20-22 (approximately lines 3947-4172) carry controls not yet reconciled in target operations documents:

- Source operational response thresholds include P0 acknowledgement ≤10 minutes and P1 ≤30 minutes. Target incident objectives are not fully mapped to these thresholds or to coverage ownership.
- Source performance targets include list/search latency below 200/300 ms and a ≤200 KB bundle budget, while target lists 350/400 ms and route bundles of 220–400 KB. Measure and choose budgets; do not report both as the same target.
- Source rollout uses 5/25/50% stages, defined bake windows, and rollback within 15 seconds; target uses statistical canary opens and remote flag propagation that can take up to 300 seconds. Map detection and rollback consequences explicitly.
- Source database recovery targets are RPO 15 minutes/RTO 2 hours, with quarterly drills; target proposes PITR RPO 5 minutes/RTO 1 hour. Object-storage and regional recovery remain unresolved. Track the source control IDs and selected targets under OPEN-23/49.
- Source requires an integration removal drill for each relevant release; target cadence differs as described above. Preserve evidence requirements when resolving cadence.

### Phases, governance, registries, and UX

Root SSOT §§23–25 (including the registers in §24.8) and Appendices A–C, plus UX patterns M-01…M-100 (Appendix D) (v6.2 numbering), have not received a complete row-level disposition.

- **Phase mapping:** the [69-row phase/gate/governance crosswalk](#phase_gate_governance_crosswalkmd) records candidate links for the 11 IP phases and 11 SSOT capability phases against target Ph0–Ph4/WPs, plus distinct exit-gate families. It is unapproved and does not equate phase numbers. Institutional exits include ≥500 imports, atomic seat operations, public certificate verification, exact revoke/reassign with progress retention, and institution isolation; OPEN-42 feature/WP alignment and OPEN-50 owner disposition remain open.
- **Governance:** the same candidate crosswalk preserves seven C1–C7 source classes and their target candidates. Source approval rights distinguish routine, schema/dependency, destructive, secrets, architecture/commercial, and locked-register changes. Target DEV/ADR/ARB procedures do not yet prove equivalent authority; record owners and required approvals before claiming governance parity.
- **Register identities:** GAP-001…038, GAP-T1…8, OD-01…53, D-01…05, and DOR-001…026 need disposition, owner/blocker, and recommendation mapping. “Resolved” labels in the source do not always establish verified closure; preserve e.g. explicitly unverified GAP items.

State inventory count also conflicts (53 vs 40). Keep both claims with their source locations until an owner resolves them.

It records 18 substantive matches, 53 partial mappings, 20 conflicts and 9 patterns with no candidate in the seven reviewed UX files. Owner disposition remains open; do not infer equivalence from visual similarity or treat "no target" as a whole-repository absence claim.

Target Host/Remote language must continue to mark federation optional under the bundled baseline.

Mark these as unverified until linked to primary sources; do not upgrade them to current legal or standards claims without verification.

### Legacy source evidence conflicts

These findings are conflicts among dated repository documents or between documentation and observed code. They are not future product requirements unless separately adopted.

- `docs/engineering/TARGET_ARCHITECTURE.md` labels several modules, including messaging, `ephemeral` and expected-empty, while `docs/engineering/DATABASE.md` documents PostgreSQL-backed threads/messages and source route inventories label thread/message routes persisted. The migration plan now treats the module label as insufficient proof of empty data; each table requires inventory and route/storage review before exclusion. OPEN-17 still determines whether a production database exists or may be inspected.

Current `apps/api/src/billing-stripe.ts` contains full-current-period refund handling, and `tests/integration/stripe-billing.test.ts` contains corresponding cases. The legacy prose is stale relative to current source code; it must remain dated evidence, not be treated as the target billing policy or current implementation description.

Current `apps/api/src/server.ts` calls `assertPlatformAdmin` on the route; `tests/integration/skill-forecasting.test.ts` covers the platform-admin path. Keep the earlier condition as historical audit evidence and treat the later fix/code as stronger current evidence.

<a id="record-adr-015-persistence-crosswalk--adr-015-persistence-crosswalk"></a>

## ADR-015 Persistence Crosswalk

Doc ID: TRC-18 · Status: Candidate mapping; not approved · Source: DOC-000133, docs/engineering/adr/ADR-015-core-loop-persistence.md.

The source ADR is dated 2026-10-10 and says “Accepted for this branch, pending owner ratification.” It describes the branch implementation decision to persist core-loop writes in PostgreSQL while retaining a boot-hydrated Map read model. The target blueprint's ADR-T05 selects PostgreSQL for all reads and writes, without an in-process Map read model. This is a current-implementation versus target-design difference; neither statement is silently substituted for the other.

The row-level source anchors and dispositions are in [ADR_015_PERSISTENCE_CROSSWALK.csv](28_TRACEABILITY_AND_COVERAGE.md#adr_015_persistence_crosswalkcsv), EVD-003073 through EVD-003082.

<a id="record-adr-015-persistence-crosswalk--reconciliation"></a>

### Reconciliation

- The ADR's write sequence is commit first, then update the Map read model. It also records per-record base checks, database predicates, and a 409 conflict response. The design explicitly says these guards prevent selected lost updates but do not prevent stale reads between API writers.
- The ADR's operating constraint is one API process per database, guarded by a PostgreSQL session advisory lock; startup refuses a second writer, and lock loss makes the API unhealthy. Out-of-band SQL changes require restart and rehydration. Boot hydration grows with core data; the ADR's exit path is SQL reads and removal of Maps before scaling replicas.
- The ADR's entity scope grew across migrations 00043, 00044 and 00046 and a later messaging addition. Its route durability statements and test results belong to that dated branch and do not establish current coverage or hosted deployment state.
- Migration 00043 includes consequential uniqueness, foreign-key and token-storage changes. The ADR requires owner ratification before applying those changes to populated data and flags duplicate case-insensitive emails. The 2026-10-23 repository audit (DOC-000171) (`31_LEGACY_ARCHIVE/docs/reports/REPOSITORY_AUDIT_AND_GITHUB_ISSUE_REPORT.md`, removed 2026-10-27; recover with `git show 85706db0:Yash_Final_Project_Document/31_LEGACY_ARCHIVE/docs/reports/REPOSITORY_AUDIT_AND_GITHUB_ISSUE_REPORT.md`) separately reports the hosted identity/schema mismatch at §A line 18, 52 local migrations at §B line 35, and migration/single-writer blockers at §D lines 69-70 and §E lines 79-82. These are dated audit statements, not current external-state verification.
- Target ADR-T05 remains the blueprint's proposed SQL-only system-of-record decision. OPEN-52 now asks the architecture, database and platform owners to approve a compatible migration/implementation bridge, acceptance evidence, and the point at which single-writer operation may be retired.

<a id="record-adr-015-persistence-crosswalk--review-limits"></a>

### Review limits

This was a full read of the ADR and a documentation cross-check against current architecture notes, target ADR-T05, data/migration documents, readiness and the later repository audit. I did not inspect migration SQL, rerun PostgreSQL tests, check CI, query a hosted database, or verify the current deployment. No implementation or migration status is upgraded by this crosswalk.
<!-- source-record-end: Yash_Final_Project_Document/28_TRACEABILITY_AND_COVERAGE/ADR_015_PERSISTENCE_CROSSWALK.md -->

## Historical Architecture Durability Report Crosswalk

Doc ID: TRC-17 · Status: Candidate mapping; not approved · Source: [DOC-000158](00_CONTROL_CENTRE.md#knowledge_preservation_manifestcsv), docs/reports/ARCHITECTURE_DURABILITY_REPORT.md.

This crosswalk maps the dated 2026-10-22 report at Git HEAD 83dc7d247efbd3b3c3312bf74ac49978d33e924 to the current target blueprint and later evidence. It records what the report asserted, where the subject belongs, and known conflicts. The report is evidence about that recorded revision, not a current security assessment, benchmark, production certification, or approval of its proposed fixes.

The row-level source anchors and dispositions are in [ARCHITECTURE_DURABILITY_REPORT_CROSSWALK.csv](28_TRACEABILITY_AND_COVERAGE.md#architecture_durability_report_crosswalkcsv), EVD-003061 through EVD-003072.

<a id="record-architecture-durability-report-crosswalk--reconciliation-notes"></a>

### Reconciliation notes

- **Persistence and route counts:** The report's 54 durable / 3 stateless / 217 ephemeral split is a distinct dated classification of 274 routes. The later audit crosswalk records 70 / 3 / 201 over 274 records, while the observed reference inventory has 278 routes. Keep these populations separate until route-level semantic disposition is approved.
- **Scale estimates:** The 2GB and tens-of-seconds projection is based on an assumed workload. It is not a measured capacity result. Current architecture documentation does identify hydrated reads and single-writer scope as trade-offs.
- **Security findings:** OTP hashing, referee expiry, RLS database role, assessment-session persistence, and AI quota claims remain historical until rechecked against current source and required tests. The target contracts specify intended controls, not implementation proof.
- **Billing:** The report's statement that Stripe was absent or simulated predates the 2026-10-23 UTC repository audit, which records Stripe backend and durable PostgreSQL billing after PR #71. This supersession concerns the code snapshot; webhook delivery, hosted schema, commercial approval, and production behavior remain separate questions.
- **Deployment:** The Oracle Caddy and image findings refer to the report's dated configuration. They need current configuration and staging evidence before they can be closed or presented as present-day defects.
- **Identity:** SEC-* and OPS-* are source-local identifiers. Do not reuse them as target RISK-_, OPEN-_, or TEST-* IDs.

<a id="record-architecture-durability-report-crosswalk--review-limits"></a>

### Review limits

This pass read the full report and mapped its major claim groups to current target documents and known later evidence. It did not re-run the report's checks, inspect the current source code for each security claim, test the running app, inspect hosted database roles, verify provider delivery, or exercise Oracle staging. Therefore the crosswalk does not mark any report finding fixed, current, or production-verified.

<a id="record-audit-documentation-crosscheck--limits-and-disposition"></a>

### Limits and disposition

The audit says its local link scan checked file targets, not heading anchors or external URLs, and explicitly did not certify whole-document semantic acceptance for these records. A manifest join establishes discoverability, not completeness or cleanup eligibility. The two missing paths (`project-maintenance.sh` and `scripts/tests/project-maintenance.test.sh`) are merely preserved as audit claims; their purpose and whether they should exist remain unresolved. No file was created or deleted. DOC-000191 and affected manifest source entries retain their existing processing statuses.

<a id="record-audit-probe-evidence-crosswalk--interpretation"></a>

### Interpretation

- `arbitrary challenge text` reports `status=passed` and `score=100` while also recording `executed=false` and `verification=unverified`. It is not a pass or verification result.
- `batch lease expiry` reports 6 jobs, 9 executions, and 3 duplicated jobs from a synthetic `MemoryJobStore` scenario with timing assumptions. It does not establish behavior with PostgreSQL or a live provider.

These are dated audit records, not current tests. No probe was rerun; the test strategy and failure analysis are candidate documentation destinations, not proof that their requirements are implemented.

<a id="record-observed-implementation-control-crosswalk--observed-implementation-control-crosswalk"></a>

## Observed Implementation Control Crosswalk

Doc ID: TRC-07 | Owner: Documentation Architect | Version: 1.0 | Status: Candidate preservation map; owner review required | Date: 2026-10-25

This crosswalk maps dated reference claims in four repository documents to candidate target documents. It separates implementation observations, source requirements, historical test results, stale prose, and future target design. It is not a code audit, current test report, adopted requirement, or approval. Every row remains an unapproved candidate. The companion [CSV](#observed_implementation_control_crosswalkcsv) preserves 38 row-level claims, source document/evidence IDs, revision and content hash, anchors, target candidates, comparison labels, and unresolved disposition.

<a id="record-observed-implementation-control-crosswalk--sources-and-provenance"></a>

### Sources and provenance

| Source                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Manifest record         | Source revision                                                  | Content SHA-256                                                    | Scope note                                                                                   |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `docs/engineering/DATABASE.md` (`31_LEGACY_ARCHIVE/docs/engineering/DATABASE.md`, removed 2026-10-27; recover with `git show 85706db0:Yash_Final_Project_Document/31_LEGACY_ARCHIVE/docs/engineering/DATABASE.md`) | DOC-000137 / EVD-000137 | `431c425a`, 2026-10-23                                           | `0879ff0703827fa3253380daca3232a45f78407fe5f21e31f92e8706a4915f82` | Static storage, migration, privacy and RLS observations; no live DB proof.                   |
| `docs/quality/OPERATIONS.md` (`31_LEGACY_ARCHIVE/docs/quality/OPERATIONS.md`, removed 2026-10-27; recover with `git show 85706db0:Yash_Final_Project_Document/31_LEGACY_ARCHIVE/docs/quality/OPERATIONS.md`)       | DOC-000152 / EVD-000152 | `2aea1085`, 2026-10-24; current-build section checked 2026-10-23 | `f4977e0a82c853064fd27137f03b8ae7fc836cba68955f6fb6b2b01c81627c18` | Sections 1-8 are a target operating model; section 9 is reference implementation/operations. |
| `docs/quality/SECURITY.md` (`31_LEGACY_ARCHIVE/docs/quality/SECURITY.md`, removed 2026-10-27; recover with `git show 85706db0:Yash_Final_Project_Document/31_LEGACY_ARCHIVE/docs/quality/SECURITY.md`)             | DOC-000153 / EVD-000153 | `2aea1085`, 2026-10-24                                           | `dda6537adf773b945e8fbd2ffae16e050902d0ae76bc392a811df6a100bb7006` | Source limits evidence scope and distinguishes requirements from assurance.                  |
| `docs/quality/TESTING.md` (`31_LEGACY_ARCHIVE/docs/quality/TESTING.md`, removed 2026-10-27; recover with `git show 85706db0:Yash_Final_Project_Document/31_LEGACY_ARCHIVE/docs/quality/TESTING.md`)                | DOC-000154 / EVD-000154 | `335e9827`, 2026-10-23                                           | `fbe7077f12d7ea0893fdc04a3a2d88b35ca3a93a4368e74fc1882df9252b2f11` | Historical baseline is dated 2026-10-21; coverage claims are not current test results.       |

<a id="record-observed-implementation-control-crosswalk--interpretation"></a>

### Interpretation

- `candidate-partial`, `candidate-conflict`, and `candidate-stale-reference` describe the comparison in named target files; they do not select future policy.
- `candidate-historical-evidence` preserves dated results. `candidate-covered` means a similar control or caveat exists, not that implementation is verified.
- `VerificationState` is conservative: current code/runtime was not revalidated for these rows in this checkpoint. The four source Markdown files were read and content hashes recorded.
- Repeated topics (erasure, RLS, delivery and export) retain distinct source evidence; they are not independent proof. Target design remains subject to owner review. Root `SSOT.md` remains unchanged.

<a id="record-observed-implementation-control-crosswalk--principal-unresolved-dispositions"></a>

### Principal unresolved dispositions

1. Reconcile single-writer API topology, Map-backed durability, worker duplicate execution and target outbox/fencing design.
2. Complete table/module durability and erasure/export inventories, retry behavior, legal holds, shared-record retention and deletion evidence.
3. Map source RLS/grant limits to target tenant policies and define evidence beyond `RLS enabled`.
4. Map provider acceptance versus delivery, queue retry/idempotency and integration incident handling.
5. Keep authentication, hashing, session and billing reference behavior separate from target designs; verify current claims against code when required.
6. Separate historical tests from target test specifications; close accessibility, performance and release-evidence gaps.
7. Route the `z.coerce.boolean()` discrepancy to a separately authorized code task; this initiative does not change source code.

<a id="record-observed-implementation-control-crosswalk--validation-limits"></a>

### Validation limits

CSV structure and row IDs can be checked mechanically; source anchors can be compared to current text. No database query, migration, app test, browser, deployment, live provider, legal review or production control was exercised. Source Markdown and target design documents remain in place; no cleanup is authorized by this map.
<!-- source-record-end: Yash_Final_Project_Document/28_TRACEABILITY_AND_COVERAGE/OBSERVED_IMPLEMENTATION_CONTROL_CROSSWALK.md -->

<a id="record-ux-pattern-source-crosswalk--ux-pattern-source-crosswalk"></a>

## UX Pattern Source Crosswalk

Doc ID: TRC-06 | Status: Candidate preservation map; owner review required | Date: 2026-10-25

The [CSV crosswalk](#ux_pattern_source_crosswalkcsv) preserves all 100 root SSOT Appendix D IDs, titles, line anchors and exact Symptom/Cost/Fix/Applies-to/Acceptance fields, alongside candidate UX destinations, relationship, and concise residuals from a read-only comparison of seven target UX files. It does not establish owner approval, implementation, or current behavior. Exact means substantive overlap, not verbatim equivalence. `No target` means none was located in the reviewed seven UX files, not that no related content exists elsewhere.

Material conflicts include loading and mutation feedback (M-01/02/18/82), progress and timeouts (M-04/06), success and confirmation patterns (M-16/17), form validation and autosave (M-21/22/24), offline action scope (M-42), Undo timing (M-47), error privacy (M-34), notification disclosure (M-52/54), stale-write status (M-66), optimistic saves (M-70), and motion duration (M-73). OPEN-50 and relevant OPEN-UX records remain unresolved.

The source says the 100 patterns should be applied through feature specifications rather than copied into each one. This crosswalk therefore provides traceability and residuals without duplicating every pattern throughout the feature corpus. Source citations embedded in the old register and its quantitative claims have not been independently researched or verified.
<!-- source-record-end: Yash_Final_Project_Document/28_TRACEABILITY_AND_COVERAGE/UX_PATTERN_SOURCE_CROSSWALK.md -->
